People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… people have been telling me I’m hard to follow. I can see them kind of losing me. That’s why I came in.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are disconnected or jumping from one idea to another when this happens?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. It’s like I start with the answer, but then it slips off into other things—like invoices, or something about garden soil, or mortgage papers. I don’t really mean to. I know it probably sounds strange, but sometimes I get the feeling certain customers aren’t really there for jewelry. I can see them kind of losing me. That’s why I came in.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are disconnected or jumping from one idea to another when this happens?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. It’s like I start with the answer, but then it slips off into other things—like invoices, or something about garden soil, or mortgage papers. I don’t really mean to. I know later it might not make sense, but when I hear my name from the polishing room, it feels real.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to stay on topic in conversations, even when you're not feeling stressed or anxious?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. It’s embarrassing. Someone asks me something simple at the shop, like when a ring repair will be ready, and I start answering but then I’m talking about invoices, or the garden soil, or the mortgage papers. I can see them getting confused, so I try to stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your thoughts are more disorganized than your speech, or is it the other way around?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can separate them. It feels like the thoughts get tangled first, and then it comes out that way. Like I said… someone asks when a ring repair will be ready, and suddenly I’m talking about invoices, garden soil, mortgage papers. Then I see I’ve lost them.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that people are actually checking up on you or plotting against you when they ask questions about the new showroom?\n\nPatient: I… yes, sometimes I do think that. I know it probably sounds odd, but when customers keep asking about the new showroom, I get convinced they’re really checking whether I’m going to miss payments. My husband says they’re just being friendly, but it doesn’t always feel that way to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you hold any strong beliefs that you think are true, even when others tell you they are not?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose so. I know it sounds odd, but when certain customers ask too much about the new showroom, I get convinced they’re really checking whether I’ll miss payments. My husband says they’re just being friendly, but I don’t always believe that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these beliefs about people checking up on you are part of a larger plan or scheme?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a whole scheme. People just… stop following.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think that there's something unusual or unreal going on that other people don't seem to notice?\n\nPatient: I… maybe. I know it probably sounds strange, but sometimes I get the feeling certain customers aren’t really there for jewelry. Like they’re checking up on whether I’ll miss payments or something, because they ask so many questions about the new showroom. My husband says they’re just being friendly, but… I don’t know.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear or see things that other people don't?\n\nPatient: Sometimes. When the shop is quiet, I’ll hear… like a woman muttering my name from the polishing room. Or I smell burning metal even when the torches are off. I usually go check, because it feels real enough.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice you hear calling your name is real?\n\nPatient: At the time, yes… or close enough that I go look. I know later it might not make sense, but when I hear my name from the polishing room, it feels real.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to stay on topic in conversations, even when you're not feeling stressed or anxious?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. It’s embarrassing. Someone asks me something simple at the shop, like when a ring repair will be ready, and I start answering but then I’m talking about invoices, or the garden soil, or the mortgage papers. I can see them getting confused, so I try to stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your thoughts are more disorganized than your speech, or is it the other way around?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can separate them. It feels like the thoughts get tangled first, and then it comes out that way. Like I said… someone asks when a ring repair will be ready, and suddenly I’m talking about invoices, garden soil, mortgage papers. Then I see I’ve lost them.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that people are actually checking up on you or plotting against you when they ask questions about the new showroom?\n\nPatient: I… yes, sometimes I do think that. I know it probably sounds odd, but when customers keep asking about the new showroom, I get convinced they’re really checking whether I’m going to miss payments. My husband says they’re just being friendly, but it doesn’t always feel that way to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you hold any strong beliefs that you think are true, even when others tell you they are not?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose so. I know it sounds odd, but when certain customers ask too much about the new showroom, I get convinced they’re really checking whether I’ll miss payments. My husband says they’re just being friendly, but I don’t always believe that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these beliefs about people checking up on you are part of a larger plan or scheme?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a whole scheme. It’s more… I get convinced some customers are coming in just to check on whether I’ll miss payments, because they keep asking about the new showroom. Even when my husband says they’re only being friendly, it doesn’t always feel that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these beliefs about people checking up on you are something you think about all the time, or just sometimes?\n\nPatient: Just sometimes. It’s not every minute or anything… but when they start asking too much about the new showroom, I can get stuck on it and think they’re really checking if I’ll miss payments. Even if my husband says they’re just being friendly.\n\nDoctor: Do you hear voices or sounds that other people can't hear when you're not alone or in a quiet place?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yes. I usually go check, because it feels real at the time.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n14\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF23, F25.0, F29\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… people have been telling me I’m hard to follow. At work, someone asks something simple, like when a ring repair will be done, and I start answering but somehow I’m talking about invoices, then garden soil, then mortgage papers. I can see them kind of losing me. That’s why I came in.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your thoughts are disconnected or jumping from one idea to another when this happens?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. It’s like I start with the answer, but then it slips off into other things—like invoices, or something about garden soil, or mortgage papers. I don’t really mean to. People just… stop following.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever think that there's something unusual or unreal going on that other people don't seem to notice?\n\nPatient: I… maybe. I know it probably sounds strange, but sometimes I get the feeling certain customers aren’t really there for jewelry. Like they’re checking up on whether I’ll miss payments or something, because they ask so many questions about the new showroom. My husband says they’re just being friendly, but… I don’t know.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever hear or see things that other people don't?\n\nPatient: Sometimes. When the shop is quiet, I’ll hear… like a woman muttering my name from the polishing room. Or I smell burning metal even when the torches are off. I usually go check, because it feels real enough.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the voice you hear calling your name is real?\n\nPatient: At the time, yes… or close enough that I go look. I know later it might not make sense, but when I hear my name from the polishing room, it feels real.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to stay on topic in conversations, even when you're not feeling stressed or anxious?\n\nPatient: Yes… I think so. It’s embarrassing. Someone asks me something simple at the shop, like when a ring repair will be ready, and I start answering but then I’m talking about invoices, or the garden soil, or the mortgage papers. I can see them getting confused, so I try to stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your thoughts are more disorganized than your speech, or is it the other way around?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can separate them. It feels like the thoughts get tangled first, and then it comes out that way. Like I said… someone asks when a ring repair will be ready, and suddenly I’m talking about invoices, garden soil, mortgage papers. Then I see I’ve lost them.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that people are actually checking up on you or plotting against you when they ask questions about the new showroom?\n\nPatient: I… yes, sometimes I do think that. I know it probably sounds odd, but when customers keep asking about the new showroom, I get convinced they’re really checking whether I’m going to miss payments. My husband says they’re just being friendly, but it doesn’t always feel that way to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you hold any strong beliefs that you think are true, even when others tell you they are not?\n\nPatient: Yes… I suppose so. I know it sounds odd, but when certain customers ask too much about the new showroom, I get convinced they’re really checking whether I’ll miss payments. My husband says they’re just being friendly, but I don’t always believe that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these beliefs about people checking up on you are part of a larger plan or scheme?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a whole scheme. It’s more… I get convinced some customers are coming in just to check on whether I’ll miss payments, because they keep asking about the new showroom. Even when my husband says they’re only being friendly, it doesn’t always feel that way.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these beliefs about people checking up on you are something you think about all the time, or just sometimes?\n\nPatient: Just sometimes. It’s not every minute or anything… but when they start asking too much about the new showroom, I can get stuck on it and think they’re really checking if I’ll miss payments. Even if my husband says they’re just being friendly.\n\nDoctor: Do you hear voices or sounds that other people can't hear when you're not alone or in a quiet place?\n\nPatient: Sometimes, yes. When the shop is quiet, I’ll hear… like a woman muttering my name from the polishing room. Or I’ll smell burning metal even when the torches are off. I usually go check, because it feels real at the time.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe these people checking up on you are part of an organized group or is it just individual customers?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s, like, an organized group. It feels more like certain customers… asking too much about the new showroom, like they’re checking whether I’m going to miss payments. I know that sounds odd.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these beliefs about people checking up on you are all connected in some way?\n\nPatient: Kind of, yes. I know it sounds strange, but when certain customers keep asking about the new showroom, it all feels connected… like they’re really checking whether I’m going to miss payments. My husband says they’re just being friendly, but I still get convinced sometimes.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

It’s like I start with the answer, but then it slips off into other things—like invoices, or something about garden soil, or mortgage papers. I don’t really mean to. But when they start asking too many questions, I can get convinced again, even if my husband says they’re just being friendly.\n\n[Last Question]\nDo you believe that the idea of customers checking up on you is a strange or unrealistic thought that you have?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 53 / jeweler_or_precious_stone_or_metal_worker / some_college\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I know it sounds odd, but I’ve gotten convinced some customers are coming in just to check whether I’ll miss payments, because they ask too many questions about the new showroom even after my husband says they’re only being friendly.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Reserved patient.\nA reserved patient may\n1) provide brief, vague, or evasive answers to questions about their symptoms and experiences,\n2) be reluctant to answer questions about personal information, emotions, or sensitive clinical experiences, and\n3) refrain from voluntarily providing information beyond what the doctor specifically asks.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },

It’s embarrassing. Someone asks me something simple at the shop, like when a ring repair will be ready, and I start answering but then I’m talking about invoices, or the garden soil, or the mortgage papers. I can see them getting confused, so I try to stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your thoughts are more disorganized than your speech, or is it the other way around?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can separate them. It feels like the thoughts get tangled first, and then it comes out that way. Like I said… someone asks when a ring repair will be ready, and suddenly I’m talking about invoices, garden soil, mortgage papers. Then I see I’ve lost them.\n\nDoctor: Do you believe that people are actually checking up on you or plotting against you when they ask questions about the new showroom?\n\nPatient: I… yes, sometimes I do think that. I know it sounds odd, but when certain customers ask too much about the new showroom, I get convinced they’re really checking whether I’ll miss payments. My husband says they’re just being friendly, but I don’t always believe that.\n\nDoctor: Do you think these beliefs about people checking up on you are part of a larger plan or scheme?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a whole scheme.
